# Codex TUI tool display - implementation reference

This report specifies the source-level rendering behavior needed to reproduce Codex's tool display in pi: **state predicates, literal text, span styles, layout algorithms, output retention, and integration boundaries**. Markdown is the canonical report; [the HTML edition](codex-tui-tool-cards.html) contains the same chapters in a navigable, offline page.

- **Codex source:** [`121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df`](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df), commit time `2026-09-07T03:19:57Z`. Research checkout: `../codex/` (not included). This revision deliberately retains that source pin rather than mixing behavior from different versions.
- **Pi comparison:** installed `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent` **0.85.1**, plus `pi-tool-display` at workspace commit `cad5b2d37f6581a687ae8366cb2b875bc13eb949`. The integration chapter records that historical baseline; see the [implementation status](implementation-decisions.md#implementation-status) for the delivered extension.
- **Evidence:** implementation and helper inspection, committed test assertions, and committed snapshots. No Codex build, live Codex capture, or pi integration execution was performed for this revision. Plain-text snapshots establish text/layout, not colors.

### Historical workspace sources

References to the “current workspace” below describe the recorded workspace commit, not the delivered extension. Workspace source links may now show newer code. Inspect the recorded version from the repository root with `git show cad5b2d37f6581a687ae8366cb2b875bc13eb949:<repository-relative-path>`. In particular, the removed `builtins.ts`, `builtins.test.ts`, `replay.ts`, and `replay.test.ts` remain available under `packages/extensions/pi-tool-display/src/` at that commit.

### Reading conventions

`"  └ "` means exactly two spaces, U+2514, one space: **four terminal columns**. `"    "` is four spaces. Quoted code literals specify actual whitespace; angle-bracket placeholders such as `<command>` denote substituted content, not literal text. `width` is the available width passed to that renderer, after any host reserve, unless explicitly stated otherwise. A **logical line**, a **wrapped viewport row**, a UTF-8 byte, a Unicode scalar, and a grapheme are different units; each limit below names its unit.

The glyphs are intentionally distinct: exec transcript success `✓` (U+2713); plan/approval success `✔` (U+2714); exec transcript/approval failure `✗` (U+2717); patch failure `✘` (U+2718); diff hunk separator `⋮` (U+22EE); omission `…` (U+2026) versus literal ASCII `...`. Family chapters specify their exact use.

Style descriptions separate foreground, background, and modifiers; the shared chapter specifies unset/reset fields and added/removed modifier-mask composition. **Default** means no explicit override, not hardcoded white/black. `DIM` is a terminal modifier, not a fixed gray. Named terminal colors, dynamically computed RGB colors, and syntax-theme colors are documented separately. Browser CSS is only document styling; it is not an authoritative Codex terminal palette.

### Implementation map

| Requirement                                                                                      | Canonical chapter                                                                            |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ANSI colors, DIM/BOLD, capability detection, animation math, syntax themes                       | [Shared terminal styles](#shared-terminal-styles)                                            |
| Event routing, completion/error normalization, grouping ownership, cancellation, replay, caching | [Lifecycle and ownership](#lifecycle-and-ownership)                                          |
| Running/Ran/You ran, Exploring/Explored, command/output gutters, 5/50-row budgets, live storage  | [Exec and exploration](#exec-and-exploration)                                                |
| Calling/Called, typed results, media notices, JSON formatting, Node REPL special cases           | [MCP calls](#mcp-calls)                                                                      |
| Added/Deleted/Edited, counts, line numbers, hunks, exact diff palettes and wrapping              | [Patches and diffs](#patches-and-diffs)                                                      |
| Web search, plans, image tools, agent collaboration, background terminals                        | [Other tool families](#other-tool-families)                                                  |
| Approvals, permission decisions, user questions, hook status                                     | [Tool interaction surfaces](#tool-interaction-surfaces)                                      |
| Existing pi renderer APIs, theme mapping, extension capabilities and gaps                        | [Pi integration](#pi-integration)                                                            |
| Literal upstream output examples and conformance cases                                           | [Snapshot examples](#snapshot-examples), [Integration conformance](#integration-conformance) |

The compact UI is family-specific. Exec/MCP completion can have a green or red **bullet**; exploration and web-search completion have a dim bullet; patches are immediately inserted diff summaries. A single global `pending → success/error` card template cannot reproduce these differences.

## Shared terminal styles

### Style values, inheritance, and semantic colors

This chapter specifies shared rendering behavior at Codex commit `121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df`. In the tables, **unset** means the style does not supply that attribute; **Reset** means an explicit `Color::Reset`. These are distinct: syntax-theme “terminal default” produces an unset foreground, whereas `best_color` on ANSI16 produces Reset. Neither means white or a particular RGB triplet. [Conversion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L485-L535), [Reset assertion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_palette.rs#L574-L580).

| Construction                                                 | Foreground                                      | Background | Added modifiers |
| ------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------- | ---------- | --------------- |
| Raw text / `Style::default()`                                | Unset; inherits surrounding/default foreground  | Unset      | None            |
| `.bold()`                                                    | Unchanged                                       | Unchanged  | `BOLD`          |
| `.dim()`                                                     | Unchanged, including an existing ANSI/RGB color | Unchanged  | `DIM`           |
| Ordinary completed command/MCP success marker `"•"` (U+2022) | `Color::Green`                                  | Unset      | `BOLD`          |
| Ordinary completed command/MCP failure marker `"•"`          | `Color::Red`                                    | Unset      | `BOLD`          |
| Completed exploration marker `"•"`                           | Unset                                           | Unset      | `DIM`           |
| Exploration operation label, such as `"Read"`                | `Color::Cyan`                                   | Unset      | None            |
| Exec transcript prefix `"$ "` = dollar + one U+0020          | `Color::Magenta`                                | Unset      | None            |

These are actual constructions, not a universal mapping from tool outcomes to colors. Family chapters specify when each construction is selected. In particular, completion does not universally mean green. Ordinary header verbs are bold spans separated by raw `" "` spans; bold does not automatically extend to the following command or invocation. [Exec constructions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/exec_cell/render.rs#L255-L389), [MCP constructions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/history_cell/mcp.rs#L135-L174), [transcript prefix](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/exec_cell/render.rs#L195-L209).

The exact low ANSI-index mapping implemented for syntax colors is:

| ANSI index | Ratatui variant                                                      |
| ---------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 0          | `Black`                                                              |
| 1          | `Red`                                                                |
| 2          | `Green`                                                              |
| 3          | `Yellow`                                                             |
| 4          | `Blue`                                                               |
| 5          | `Magenta`                                                            |
| 6          | `Cyan`                                                               |
| 7          | `Gray`, corresponding to ANSI foreground code 37                     |
| 8–255      | `Indexed(n)`, including 8–15; not converted to named bright variants |

Do not replace the named 0–7 variants with `Indexed(0..7)`: the source explicitly preserves named-color bold/bright treatment. The terminal theme determines these colors’ appearance; the first sixteen RGB entries in Codex’s Xterm table are expressly illustrative. [Mapping and rationale](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L458-L482), [terminal-theme caveat](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_palette.rs#L293-L318).

Codex’s style guide prefers default primary text, bold headers, dim secondary text, cyan indicators, green success, red failure, and magenta Codex accents. It is guidance, not an exhaustive renderer specification: syntax colors, shimmer RGB, ANSI-bearing output, and diff backgrounds are explicit implementation exceptions. `DIM` is a terminal modifier, not an RGB gray or a browser opacity value. [Guide](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/styles.md#L1-L21), [output modifier application](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/exec_cell/render.rs#L129-L168).

The terminal writer diffs modifiers into Crossterm attributes: `BOLD→Bold`, `DIM→Dim`, `ITALIC→Italic`, `UNDERLINED→Underlined`, `REVERSED→Reverse`, `CROSSED_OUT→CrossedOut`, and slow/rapid blink into their corresponding attributes. Removing bold or dim emits `NormalIntensity`; removing bold reapplies Dim when required by the target style. Foreground/background and attributes are explicitly reset after a draw batch. This is stateful terminal output, not an independently painted CSS span per token. [Writer](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/custom_terminal.rs#L691-L834).

Two modifier-emission exceptions apply in both the active-buffer writer and the native scrollback writer, so retained span modifiers do not guarantee terminal appearance:

- `HIDDEN` has no add/remove branch in `ModifierDiff::queue`: the writer emits neither conceal nor reveal attributes and still prints the cell symbol. The locked ANSI parser can supply HIDDEN through SGR conceal; compact exec adds DIM without removing it. See **Delegated color detection and ANSI parsing** for the dependency’s exact SGR rules.
- On a `BOLD | DIM → BOLD` transition, removing DIM emits `NormalIntensity`, resetting terminal intensity. BOLD is absent from `added = to - from`, so the writer does not re-emit it, yet records the target modifier set as BOLD.

These are Codex writer behaviors, not proposed fixes. An integration that honors HIDDEN or reasserts retained BOLD after removing DIM differs from this writer. [Active-buffer modifier diff](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/custom_terminal.rs#L777-L837), [native scrollback modifier diff](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/insert_history.rs#L386-L445), [state tracking and symbol printing](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/custom_terminal.rs#L719-L744), [DIM composition](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/exec_cell/render.rs#L129-L168).

### Palette detection, overrides, and color arithmetic

`stdout_color_level()` classifies cached `supports_color::Stream::Stdout` results in this order: `has_16m→TrueColor`; otherwise `has_256→Ansi256`; otherwise any result→Ansi16; no result→Unknown. `effective_stdout_color_level()` then applies these **presence-based** overrides; the values of `WT_SESSION` and `FORCE_COLOR` are not parsed here. [Implementation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_palette.rs#L6-L75).

| Condition, in priority order                                                           | Effective level                     |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- |
| Thread-local test palette is installed                                                 | TrueColor                           |
| `WT_SESSION` exists and `FORCE_COLOR` does not                                         | TrueColor, regardless of base level |
| Base is Ansi16, detected terminal is WindowsTerminal, and `FORCE_COLOR` does not exist | TrueColor                           |
| Otherwise                                                                              | Base stdout level                   |

`best_color(target)` uses that effective level; `best_color_for_level(target, level)` uses the supplied level directly. TrueColor returns `Color::Rgb(r,g,b)` unchanged. Ansi256 chooses minimum perceptual distance among indices **16–255 only**. Ansi16 and Unknown return Reset, not a nearest ANSI16 approximation. The public RGB/indexed constructors themselves perform no capability checks. [Selection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_palette.rs#L23-L89), [override tests](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_palette.rs#L566-L619).

The fixed candidate table can be reproduced as index `16+36r+6g+b`, with `r,g,b∈0..5` selecting component values `[0,95,135,175,215,255]`, followed by grayscale index `232+k` with each component `8+10k`, `k∈0..23`. These are the helper’s assumed candidates, not a guarantee that a terminal has never reprogrammed them. [Committed table](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_palette.rs#L317-L559).

Default foreground/background are discovered separately from capability level. On Unix, the probe sends the exact concatenated bytes `"\x1b]10;?\x1b\\\x1b]11;?\x1b\\"`; both replies must be present. The default budget is **100 ms per probe group**, not 100 ms per color. Replies accept BEL (`\x07`) or ST (`\x1b\\`) termination and case-insensitive `rgb:`/`rgba:` with exactly three/four slash-separated components. Each component has 1–4 hexadecimal digits and converts by integer division `value*255/(16^digits−1)`; RGBA alpha is validated but discarded. Thus `rgb:ffff/8000/0000` becomes `(255,127,0)`, not `(255,128,0)`. [Query and pairing](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_probe.rs#L242-L255), [timeout](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_probe.rs#L23-L33), [parser and assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_probe.rs#L719-L852).

Windows prefers OSC replies and otherwise reads `GetConsoleScreenBufferInfoEx`: foreground index `attributes & 0x0f`, background index `(attributes>>4)&0x0f`; each COLORREF decodes low byte red, next green, next blue. Reverse-video attributes do not swap these discovered defaults. [Windows probe](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_probe/windows.rs#L30-L75), [console decoding](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_probe/windows.rs#L294-L323).

The Unix/Windows caches remember attempted-but-unavailable results as well as successful pairs. Startup can seed either result; subsequent reads do not repeatedly probe a missing palette. `default_fg()`/`default_bg()` project the cached pair. Unsupported-platform and ordinary Unix-test implementations return no colors unless the test override supplies them. There is no live palette subscription in these helpers. [Cache and platform branches](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_palette.rs#L93-L291).

Shared arithmetic uses `f32` throughout:

- `is_light((r,g,b))`: `0.299r + 0.587g + 0.114b > 128.0`; equality is dark.
- `blend(fg,bg,a)`: each component is `(fg*a + bg*(1−a)) as u8`; no rounding-to-nearest or gamma correction, and no clamp inside this helper.
- Perceptual distance: normalize sRGB by 255; linearize with `c/12.92` for `c≤0.04045`, else `((c+0.055)/1.055)^2.4`. XYZ rows are `(0.4124,0.3576,0.1805)`, `(0.2126,0.7152,0.0722)`, `(0.0193,0.1192,0.9505)`. Divide XYZ by D65 `(0.95047,1,1.08883)`; apply `f(t)=t^(1/3)` above `0.008856`, else `7.787t+16/116`. Lab is `(116fy−16,500(fx−fy),200(fy−fz))`; return Euclidean Lab distance. [All arithmetic](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/color.rs#L1-L75).

### Motion primitives and exact timing

`tui.animations` has a deserialization default of `true`; the motion helper maps the supplied boolean directly to Animated/Reduced. It does not independently inspect an OS reduced-motion setting. There is no pending/success/error/abort/replay state in the primitive: callers supply a mode, optional start time, and explicit reduced-motion fallback. [Configuration](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/config/src/types.rs#L723-L732), [mode and API](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/motion.rs#L16-L79).

| Primitive condition                                            | Exact spans returned; foreground/background unset unless stated |
| -------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Activity, Reduced + Hidden                                     | `None`; no glyph or reserved space from the helper              |
| Activity, Reduced + StaticBullet                               | One `"•"` span, `DIM`                                           |
| Activity, Animated, raw stdout capability has truecolor        | First span of `shimmer_spans("•")`; RGB foreground + `BOLD`     |
| Activity, Animated, no truecolor, `floor(elapsed_ms/600)` even | `"•"`, no modifiers                                             |
| Activity, Animated, no truecolor, that quotient odd            | `"◦"` (U+25E6), `DIM`                                           |
| Shimmer text, Reduced, nonempty                                | One entire-text raw span; no bold/dim added                     |
| Shimmer text, empty, either mode                               | Empty span vector                                               |

No-truecolor activity has a 1,200 ms full cycle; absent start time means zero elapsed and therefore always the first phase. Truecolor activity ignores its supplied start time for animation phase. Crucially, motion reads **raw** cached `supports_color.has_16m`, not `effective_stdout_color_level`; Windows palette promotion does not automatically enable RGB shimmer. The reduced-mode assertions compare complete spans, proving styles as well as glyphs. [Branching and tests](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/motion.rs#L38-L118).

For nonempty animated shimmer, let `n` be Rust `text.chars().count()` - Unicode scalars, not graphemes or terminal columns. A lazy global `Instant` starts on the first nonempty shimmer call; despite the `PROCESS_START` name, it is not captured at executable launch. Elapsed seconds `s` come from `Duration::as_secs_f32()`. All floating-point constants, conversions, and intermediates below are **f32**, including `PI = std::f32::consts::PI`; preserve division before multiplication inside the cosine:

```text
padding = 10
period = n + 20
position = floor(((s mod 2.0) / 2.0) * (period as f32))
distance(i) = abs((i + 10) - position) as f32
t(i) = 0.5 * (1.0 + cos(PI * (distance(i) / 5.0))) if distance(i) <= 5.0
       0.0 otherwise
```

`position` represents the source’s nonnegative f32-to-`usize` truncation; the scalar-index subtraction occurs in integers before conversion to f32.

One span is emitted per scalar. On truecolor stdout, set foreground to `blend(default_bg or (255,255,255), default_fg or (128,128,128), clamp(t,0,1)*0.9)` and add `BOLD` **even outside the band**. Background stays unset: the background RGB participates in the foreground calculation only. Without truecolor, leave both colors unset and choose `DIM` for `t<0.2`, no modifier for `0.2≤t<0.6`, `BOLD` for `t≥0.6`. [Entire shimmer algorithm](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/shimmer.rs#L14-L80).

Worked derivation: for a single bullet with unavailable palette, `s=0` gives position 0, distance 10, RGB `(128,128,128)` + BOLD; `s=1` gives position 10, distance 0, RGB `(242,242,242)` + BOLD. This is arithmetic from the preceding code, not an observed screenshot. For a discovered dark background, the same sweep moves the foreground toward that dark background rather than toward fixed white.

Precision counterexample: at band center (`t=1`), background `(0,0,0)` and foreground `(250,250,250)` produce `(25,25,25)` with the source’s f32 blend. Ordinary double-precision evaluation followed by truncation gives `(24,24,24)`, because `250 * (1 - 0.9)` is just below 25. This is a source-derived arithmetic example, checked with a read-only Python calculation forcing IEEE-754 single-precision rounding, not a Codex execution. **Integration guidance:** a JavaScript port must preserve f32 rounding rather than evaluate the displayed expressions solely with doubles. [f32 shimmer blend call](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/shimmer.rs#L45-L58), [component arithmetic and casts](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/color.rs#L7-L12).

Motion helpers schedule no frames. The status widget requests another frame after **32 ms** when animated; reduced mode requests **1,000 ms** only when its display timer is running. Its Hidden fallback also omits the following `" "`. The shared frame limiter imposes a minimum **8,333,334 ns** between draw notifications, not a promise that every widget renders at 120 FPS. MCP’s active transcript cache tick is `floor(elapsed_ms/50)` and is absent when completed or animations are disabled. Freezing exec/MCP snapshots disables animation rather than choosing a new success/error color; lifecycle/replay routing remains family-specific. [Status caller](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/status_indicator_widget.rs#L219-L235), [scheduling](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/status_indicator_widget.rs#L282-L298), [limiter](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/tui/frame_rate_limiter.rs#L12-L35), [MCP tick](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/history_cell/mcp.rs#L315-L320), [MCP freeze](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/history_cell/mcp.rs#L117-L125), [exec freeze](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/exec_cell/model.rs#L178-L184).

### Syntax themes, style conversion, and guardrails

`tui.theme` is an optional case-sensitive kebab-case name. Resolution tries the bundled-name match first, then `$CODEX_HOME/themes/<name>.tmTheme`, then the adaptive default: **catppuccin-latte** when discovered background satisfies `is_light`, otherwise **catppuccin-mocha**, including unknown background. Grammar/theme data come from locked `two-face 0.5.1`, not a Codex-wide “keyword RGB” constant. The 32 bundled identifiers are `1337`, `ansi`, `base16`, `base16-256`, `base16-eighties-dark`, `base16-mocha-dark`, `base16-ocean-dark`, `base16-ocean-light`, `catppuccin-frappe`, `catppuccin-latte`, `catppuccin-macchiato`, `catppuccin-mocha`, `coldark-cold`, `coldark-dark`, `dark-neon`, `dracula`, `github`, `gruvbox-dark`, `gruvbox-light`, `inspired-github`, `monokai-extended`, `monokai-extended-bright`, `monokai-extended-light`, `monokai-extended-origin`, `nord`, `one-half-dark`, `one-half-light`, `solarized-dark`, `solarized-light`, `sublime-snazzy`, `two-dark`, and `zenburn`. [Configuration](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/config/src/types.rs#L803-L808), [resolution](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L146-L235), [identifier list](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L420-L454), [dependency pin](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/Cargo.lock#L16070-L16079).

Startup override/home persistence is write-once; subsequent calls cannot replace those saved values but update an already-initialized runtime theme. Theme swaps increment an atomic revision; `configured_theme_name()` deliberately reports persisted/default selection, not a transient preview. Unknown names or invalid custom files yield user-facing fallback warnings; repeated override persistence only logs debug. [Lifecycle](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L91-L143), [revision](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L238-L271), [configured name](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L333-L350).

| Syntect input                              | Ratatui output                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Foreground alpha `0x00`                    | Red byte is an ANSI index; use mapping above; ignore green/blue bytes |
| Foreground alpha `0x01`                    | Foreground unset, **not** explicit Reset                              |
| Foreground alpha `0xff` or any other alpha | Exact `Rgb(r,g,b)`; alpha is not opacity                              |
| Any syntax background                      | Omitted                                                               |
| FontStyle BOLD                             | Add `Modifier::BOLD`                                                  |
| FontStyle ITALIC or UNDERLINE              | Omitted; neither emitted nor converted into another modifier          |

There is no stdout-depth quantization in this conversion: an RGB theme still produces RGB spans on an ANSI16-classified terminal. ANSI themes preserve palette semantics instead. The committed conversion assertion uses foreground `(255,128,0,255)`, black background, BOLD+ITALIC and requires RGB `(255,128,0)`, unset background, BOLD without italic/underline. [Converter](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L485-L535), [style assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L931-L1075).

For the test snippet `fn main() { let answer = 42; println!("hello"); }\n`, the observed unique **explicit foreground subsets** are: `ansi` = Blue/Green/Magenta/Yellow; `base16` = Blue/Gray/Green/Indexed(9)/Magenta; `base16-256` = Blue/Gray/Green/Indexed(16)/Magenta. These are not complete theme palettes or token-to-color tables. Unlike plain snapshots, this fixture serializes actual `span.style.fg` debug values. [Extraction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L801-L817), [fixture](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/snapshots/codex_tui__render__highlight__tests__ansi_family_foreground_palette.snap#L1-L21).

Lookup patches `csharp/c-sharp→c#`, `cu/cuh/cppm/cxxm/ixx→cpp`, `golang→go`, `python3→python`, `shell→bash`, using lowercase for alias matching. It then tries token, exact syntax name, case-insensitive syntax name, and original input as extension. Highlighting preserves parser state across source lines. Reject empty input, unknown syntax, parser errors, total UTF-8 bytes **>524,288**, Rust `lines()` count **>10,000**, or any line’s bytes **>4,096**; equality is allowed. Styled-span API returns `None`; line API returns plain `code.lines()` and guarantees one empty line for empty input. A trailing newline creates no phantom final line. Successful conversion removes trailing CR/LF from span fragments and retains an empty raw span for an empty line. [Lookup and limits](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L541-L661), [fallback APIs](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L679-L718).

Streaming code highlighting accepts only newline-terminated appends. **For an active highlighted state**, theme revision changes, parse failure, checked-counter overflow, or crossing a guard return `None`, requiring full-fence rerender, including removal of earlier colors after a size limit is crossed. Initialization checks the theme revision before unknown-language/oversized-input fallback; that fallback stores a permanent plain-text state (`state=None`). After checking newline termination, `append` in the permanent plain state immediately returns plain appended lines: it does not recheck the theme revision, update byte/line counters, or enforce highlighting size limits. [Streaming state machine](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight_streaming.rs#L16-L110), [initialization order](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight_streaming.rs#L36-L53), [append branch order](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight_streaming.rs#L73-L99).

The diff chapter owns the exact diff palette and background composition. Shared highlighting separately exposes theme scope backgrounds (`markup.inserted/deleted`, then `diff.inserted/deleted`); this does not change ordinary syntax spans’ unset backgrounds. [Diff interface](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L274-L315).

### ANSI ingestion and rich/transcript/raw projections

`ansi_escape_line(s)` replaces every tab with exactly `"    "` - four U+0020 spaces, **not tab-stop alignment** - then invokes the ANSI parser. Zero parsed lines yields an empty line; multiple lines log a warning and return only the first. `ansi_escape(s)` itself does not expand tabs: it calls `ansi_to_tui::IntoText` and returns styled text; either exposed parser error branch logs and panics, rather than falling back to literal escape text. Each call constructs a fresh parse; Codex’s line helper carries no SGR state between calls. The locked dependency subsection **Delegated color detection and ANSI parsing** specifies accepted escapes and tolerant malformed-input handling; the exposed error branches alone do not establish which inputs reach a panic. [Complete helper](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/ansi-escape/src/lib.rs#L1-L58).

| Projection                      | Shared styling consequence                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Exec compact output             | Parse ANSI, insert optional prefix, then add DIM to **every span**, preserving parsed foreground/background and other modifiers |
| Exec transcript output          | Parse ANSI and adaptively wrap; no blanket DIM pass                                                                             |
| Exec raw output                 | Build transcript at `u16::MAX`, then `plain_lines`: concatenate each line’s span contents into one fresh unstyled line          |
| Generic `raw_lines_from_source` | No ANSI parsing; split on LF, remove one trailing split element when source ends in LF; empty source produces no lines          |

Thus “raw” is a family-defined projection, not necessarily original subprocess bytes. A compact red ANSI span remains red plus DIM; its transcript counterpart retains red without Codex adding DIM; the exec raw counterpart retains visible text without that style. Other families’ ANSI ingestion and output budgets belong in their respective chapters. [Compact](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/exec_cell/render.rs#L129-L168), [transcript/raw](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/exec_cell/render.rs#L195-L243), [plain/source helpers](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/history_cell/mod.rs#L147-L175).

In locked `ratatui-core 0.1.2`, `Line::from(String)` calls `Line::raw`, which maps Rust `str::lines()` fragments to adjacent default-style spans **within one line**, inserting no separator text. LF and CRLF separators disappear; `Line::from("")` has zero spans. Consequently `plain_lines` constructs a fresh default-style **line**, not invariably one span: concatenated text can produce zero, one, or multiple spans. `Line::from(Span)` instead keeps that supplied span and its text, including line separators before subsequent wrapping. [Locked dependency](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/Cargo.lock#L12454-L12473), [owned-string splitting](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/text/line.rs#L222-L226), [raw constructor](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/text/line.rs#L251-L258), [conversion implementations](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/text/line.rs#L605-L629).

### Width, styled wrapping, and truncation

Codex’s corrected terminal-cell measurement `display_width(text)` is `UnicodeWidthStr::width(text)` **plus one for each U+FF9E/U+FF9F halfwidth sound mark**, returning `usize`. Ratatui’s corresponding bounded API is **`CellWidth::cell_width()`**, which returns `u16` and adds those marks. `char_width` returns one for those marks, otherwise `UnicodeWidthChar::width().unwrap_or(0)`. Codex’s `line_width` sums corrected display width separately per span. These functions do not parse ANSI escapes. `usable_content_width(total,reserved)` returns positive `total−reserved` or `None`; zero is never successful. [Width helpers and assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/width.rs#L15-L89), [line width](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/line_truncation.rs#L7-L11), [bounded Ratatui cell width](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/buffer/cell_width.rs#L19-L44).

Ratatui’s **`Line::width()` is different**: it returns a `usize` sum of `Span::width()` values, each using the content’s unadjusted Unicode width without Codex’s halfwidth-mark correction. It is neither the bounded `CellWidth::cell_width()` API nor Codex’s `line_width()`. MCP uses this unadjusted `Line::width()` for both header reservation and the inline-invocation fit test; subsequent wrapping uses Codex’s corrected helpers. [Ratatui line width](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/text/line.rs#L441-L443), [span-width sum](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/text/line.rs#L568-L575), [unadjusted span width](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/text/span.rs#L379-L386), [MCP fit decision](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/history_cell/mcp.rs#L171-L190), [wrapper measurement](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L881-L915).

`RtOptions::new(width)` defaults to LF, empty styled indents, `break_words=true`, `WordSeparator::new()`, **FirstFit**, and **HyphenSplitter**. Ordinary styled wrapping:

1. Flatten span contents and retain byte ranges plus each span style.
2. Wrap at `max(1, width−line_width(initial_indent))`, using Codex’s corrected span-width sum; take the first source range.
3. Skip following ASCII spaces, then wrap remaining source at `max(1, width−line_width(subsequent_indent))`.
4. Slice original spans by source ranges, preserving styles; patch content spans with the original line style. Use that line style on the reconstructed line. Indent spans retain their own styles. Output alignment comes from the indent line, rather than explicitly restoring source alignment.
5. For multiple input lines, initial indent applies only to the first input line; all later lines start with subsequent indent. Empty content returns the initial indent.

These minimum-one rules can overflow a nominal width: the committed width-4/indent-`">>>>"` example renders `">>>>h"`, then `"--el"`, `"--lo"`. A caller requiring prefix-only behavior must use the usable-width guard before wrapping. [Defaults and algorithm](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L798-L934), [multi-line and slicing](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L1202-L1270), [overflow assertion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L1393-L1404).

`wrap_ranges_trim` supplies source byte ranges without trailing whitespace or cursor sentinel. `wrap_ranges`, used for cursor logic, additionally includes following ASCII spaces and a **+1 sentinel byte**; those ranges can overlap or end past source length. Materialized textwrap output is mapped back after stripping synthetic indentation and ignoring an inserted trailing hyphen penalty. Halfwidth-mark graphemes use width-equivalent `界`/`a` placeholders joined by U+2060, source-offset mappings, and indivisible grapheme boundaries; placeholders never reach displayed text. [Range mapping](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L227-L396), [halfwidth projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L42-L225).

Adaptive wrapping detects URLs across concatenated spans, independently per input line:

| Classification                     | Wrapping behavior                                                                                                                        |
| ---------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| No URL-like token                  | Ordinary algorithm, caller options unchanged                                                                                             |
| URLs plus decorative markers only  | ASCII-space separator, NoHyphenation, `break_words=false`                                                                                |
| URL plus substantive non-URL token | Dedicated first-fit token wrapper; preserve URL whole, wrap normal words, split oversized non-URL tokens at extended grapheme boundaries |

A URL wider than the available row, or an indivisible oversized grapheme, is allowed to overflow. Mixed wrapping uses the indent widths but its own token algorithm rather than the caller’s splitter/break-words settings. A substantive token contains an alphanumeric character after punctuation trimming and is not a decorative marker. [Selection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L688-L761), [mixed algorithm](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L948-L1118), [marker rules](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L471-L505).

Detection splits ASCII whitespace and trims surrounding `()[]{}<>,.;:!'"`. Absolute URLs require `://`; known web/FTP/WebSocket schemes require a parsed host, other successfully parsed schemes are accepted, and parse failure falls back to `[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*://` plus nonempty remainder. Bare URLs require `/`, `?`, or `#` unless the host starts `www.`; hosts may be localhost, four valid u8 IPv4 parts, or domain labels with a 2–63 ASCII-letter TLD. This is **not a TLD registry lookup**. Ports must parse as u16 with at most five digits. Bare bracketed IPv6 is unsupported; `src/main.rs` is not a URL. [Exact detector](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L437-L680).

Worked styled example at width 10: spans `"see "` Red, `"https://exa"` Cyan, `"mple.com/path"` Magenta, `" now"` Green become three lines: `"see"` Red; the whole URL with its original Cyan/Magenta boundary; `"now"` Green. The URL line exceeds width 10 intentionally. Later viewport `Paragraph` wrapping with `Wrap { trim:false }` measures its actual rows; logical-line count alone is insufficient. [Full-object assertion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/wrapping.rs#L1630-L1648), [viewport measurement](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/history_cell/mod.rs#L216-L265).

**Default height contract.** Both `desired_height_for_mode` (rich or raw) and `desired_transcript_height` measure with `Paragraph.wrap(Wrap { trim:false }).line_count(width)`, then convert to `u16` using **`.try_into().unwrap_or(0)`**. A measured height of **65,536 or greater reports 0** - not 65,535, an unlimited height, or an error. The returned line vector remains unchanged; this is height-conversion overflow, not content truncation. Unlimited exploration accumulation, expanded exec output, and independently budgeted MCP blocks remain subject to this default reported-height contract. [Exact conversions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/history_cell/mod.rs#L223-L264).

`prefix_lines` adds one caller-supplied span per line, choosing initial/subsequent prefix, retains the source line style and span styles, but does not wrap or preserve alignment. Borrowed/owned copy helpers preserve line style, alignment, and individual span styles. Prefix literals therefore belong to callers; no shared helper implies a universal gutter width or color. [Helpers](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/line_utils.rs#L4-L76).

Styled truncation keeps complete spans until the budget would be exceeded, then takes complete extended graphemes from that span and stops. Zero-width spans are retained when reached, even after the width budget is filled; width zero returns a fresh empty unstyled line. Ellipsis truncation runs only on overflow, reserves one column, and appends **`"…"` (U+2026)** with the last retained span style, or default style if none remains. It preserves line style/alignment except when a zero-width inner truncation returns a fresh line. Example: `"abｶﾞc"` has width 5; width 3 truncation yields `"ab"`, width 4 yields `"abｶﾞ"`. [Algorithms](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/line_truncation.rs#L13-L101), [assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/line_truncation_tests.rs#L1-L19).

Do not confuse this with `truncate_text`: that helper budgets graphemes, reserves three positions for literal **`"..."`**, and for limits 0–2 simply retains that many graphemes without dots. `format_and_truncate_tool_result` uses `(max_lines*line_width).saturating_sub(max_lines)` as an approximate grapheme budget after optional JSON compaction; it is not exact terminal-row truncation. [Text helpers](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/text_formatting.rs#L17-L112).

### Integration roles and evidence boundaries

**Proposed Pi integration vocabulary, not Codex APIs:** retain separate roles for default text, strong text, secondary modifier, named terminal color, indexed color, literal RGB, activity animation, syntax foreground, and parsed ANSI style. Each role must preserve foreground/background independently and allow modifier composition. Do not bake an HTML or Pi theme’s hex values into claims about Codex’s terminal colors. The per-family chapters determine which roles apply to pending/completed/failed/interrupted/replayed cells; the diff chapter determines diff backgrounds.

The locked dependency subsection **Delegated color detection and ANSI parsing** specifies the investigated `supports-color` environment/TTY precedence and `ansi-to-tui` escape/SGR acceptance rules. Codex delegates rather than reimplements them; its explicit WT_SESSION/FORCE_COLOR overrides and parser call/error handling above compose with those dependency rules, not a separate Codex NO_COLOR/COLORTERM policy or complete terminal emulator. **Default syntax theme data** below supplies both decoded default palettes and all ordered scope rules. Actual token colors still depend on the selected grammar and scope stack; custom themes and ANSI-oriented themes are not interchangeable with those two RGB defaults. [Capability delegation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_palette.rs#L14-L20), [ANSI delegation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/ansi-escape/src/lib.rs#L40-L57), [theme initialization](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/render/highlight.rs#L69-L71).

### Delegated color detection and ANSI parsing

#### Locked implementation and evidence

Codex delegates these behaviors to published crates. Codex links below pin dependency selection at `121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df`; dependency links pin the upstream commits recorded in the corresponding crate archives. The downloaded archives’ SHA-256 values matched `Cargo.lock`, and the principal implementation files were compared byte-for-byte with those upstream commits. These are dependency implementation rules, not proposed Pi behavior.

| Dependency       | Locked version and Codex evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Implementation commit                                                                                                                                              |
| ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `supports-color` | **3.0.2**; [workspace selection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/Cargo.toml#L470-L471), [lock record](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/Cargo.lock#L14793-L14800) | [`26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6`](https://github.com/zkat/supports-color/blob/26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6/Cargo.toml#L1-L17)               |
| `ansi-to-tui`    | **8.0.1**; [lock record](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/Cargo.lock#L348-L360)                                                                                                                                         | [`585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3`](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/Cargo.toml#L1-L31)               |
| `is_ci`          | **1.2.0**; [lock record](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/Cargo.lock#L9361-L9365)                                                                                                                                       | [`5e7959455a90cf9a00cafbbd533ea25d747a07d3`](https://github.com/zkat/is_ci/blob/5e7959455a90cf9a00cafbbd533ea25d747a07d3/src/lib.rs#L25-L70)                       |
| `nom`            | **8.0.0**, used by this ANSI parser; [lock record](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/Cargo.lock#L10297-L10304)                                                                                                           | [`2cec1b3e4c9ccac62c902d60c00de6d1549ccbe1`](https://github.com/rust-bakery/nom/blob/2cec1b3e4c9ccac62c902d60c00de6d1549ccbe1/src/character/complete.rs#L753-L867) |
| `ratatui-core`   | **0.1.2**, shared style representation; [lock record](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/Cargo.lock#L12454-L12474)                                                                                                        | [`e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add`](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/style.rs#L298-L321) |

The separate locked `supports-color 2.1.0` is not the workspace version used for TUI palette detection. ANSI ingestion uses the owned `into_text()` path; the crate’s default features enable `simd` UTF-8 validation and the unused optional borrowing API `zero-copy`. [Other version](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/Cargo.lock#L14783-L14800), [Codex caller](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/ansi-escape/src/lib.rs#L40-L57), [feature defaults](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/Cargo.toml#L15-L31).

#### `supports-color`: exact environment and TTY precedence

Every environment read here uses `std::env::var`: absent **or non-Unicode** values count as read errors. Comparisons are case-sensitive; values are not trimmed. First compute `force_color` using the following table. A successfully read `FORCE_COLOR` prevents consulting `CLICOLOR_FORCE`, even when its result is zero. [Force/no-color implementation](https://github.com/zkat/supports-color/blob/26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6/src/lib.rs#L36-L59).

| First applicable case                                      | `force_color`                                                                  |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `FORCE_COLOR` is `"true"` or `""`                          | 1                                                                              |
| `FORCE_COLOR` is `"false"`                                 | 0                                                                              |
| Any other readable `FORCE_COLOR`                           | Parse Rust `usize`; parse failure gives 1; clamp successful value to at most 3 |
| `FORCE_COLOR` unreadable; readable `CLICOLOR_FORCE != "0"` | 1, including `""` and `"false"`                                                |
| Otherwise                                                  | 0                                                                              |

Thus `FORCE_COLOR="2"` forces exactly level 2 rather than establishing a minimum; `"99"` gives 3; `"-1"`, `"False"`, and `" 2"` give 1. **`FORCE_COLOR="0"` and `"false"` do not disable automatic detection.** They merely fail to force a positive level. The main procedure returns the first matching row below. [Ordered branches](https://github.com/zkat/supports-color/blob/26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6/src/lib.rs#L90-L117).

| Priority | Predicate                                                                                                                                             | Result level                           |
| -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| 1        | `force_color > 0`                                                                                                                                     | `force_color`; bypass all later checks |
| 2        | Readable `NO_COLOR` other than `"0"`; **or** `TERM == "dumb"`; **or** selected stream is not a TTY and no readable `IGNORE_IS_TERMINAL != "0"` exists | 0                                      |
| 3        | `COLORTERM` exactly `"truecolor"` or `"24bit"`; **or** `TERM` ends with `"direct"` or `"truecolor"`; **or** `TERM_PROGRAM == "iTerm.app"`             | 3                                      |
| 4        | `TERM_PROGRAM == "Apple_Terminal"`; **or** `TERM` ends with `"256"` or `"256color"`                                                                   | 2                                      |
| 5        | Any readable `COLORTERM`; **or** platform basic-ANSI predicate below; **or** readable `CLICOLOR != "0"`; **or** `is_ci::uncached()`                   | 1                                      |
| 6        | Otherwise                                                                                                                                             | 0                                      |

`NO_COLOR=""` and `NO_COLOR="false"` therefore disable detection unless positive forcing wins; `NO_COLOR="0"` does not. `IGNORE_IS_TERMINAL=""` or `"false"` bypasses only the TTY test, not `NO_COLOR` or `TERM=dumb`. Truecolor environment hints and CI detection cannot independently bypass a non-TTY stream. `CLICOLOR="0"` does not veto other positive hints. [Boolean handling and ordering](https://github.com/zkat/supports-color/blob/26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6/src/lib.rs#L54-L117).

The platform basic-ANSI predicate is deliberately broad:

- **Non-Windows:** a readable `TERM` exists and is not `"dumb"`; even `TERM=""` qualifies.
- **Windows:** unreadable `TERM` qualifies; readable `TERM` qualifies unless exactly `"dumb"` or `"cygwin"`. Other row-5 hints can still enable basic color for `cygwin`.

Suffix matching requires no particular terminal-name prefix: `TERM="256"` qualifies for level 2. The TTY predicate is exactly `std::io::stdout().is_terminal()` or `stderr().is_terminal()` for the selected stream. This dependency does not inspect terminal versions, `WT_SESSION`, command-line color flags, or terminfo, and performs no color-query escape exchange. Codex’s subsequent Windows Terminal promotions remain separate. [TTY boundary](https://github.com/zkat/supports-color/blob/26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6/src/lib.rs#L82-L117), [platform/suffix predicates](https://github.com/zkat/supports-color/blob/26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6/src/lib.rs#L119-L152), [Codex overrides](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/tui/src/terminal_palette.rs#L40-L75).

`is_ci::uncached()` returns true for `CI` exactly `"true"`, `"1"`, or `"woodpecker"`; readable `NODE` ending with `"//heroku/node/bin/node"`; or **any readable value**, including empty/`"false"`, of these exact names:

```text
CI_NAME GITHUB_ACTION GITLAB_CI NETLIFY TRAVIS CODEBUILD_SRC_DIR
BUILDER_OUTPUT GITLAB_DEPLOYMENT NOW_GITHUB_DEPLOYMENT NOW_BUILDER
BITBUCKET_DEPLOYMENT GERRIT_PROJECT SYSTEM_TEAMFOUNDATIONCOLLECTIONURI
BITRISE_IO BUDDY_WORKSPACE_ID BUILDKITE CIRRUS_CI APPVEYOR CIRCLECI
SEMAPHORE DRONE DSARI TDDIUM STRIDER TASKCLUSTER_ROOT_URL JENKINS_URL
bamboo.buildKey GO_PIPELINE_NAME HUDSON_URL WERCKER MAGNUM NEVERCODE
RENDER SAIL_CI SHIPPABLE
```

The spelling is `GITHUB_ACTION`, singular; `CI="false"` does not override another qualifying variable. [Complete CI predicate](https://github.com/zkat/is_ci/blob/5e7959455a90cf9a00cafbbd533ea25d747a07d3/src/lib.rs#L25-L70).

Level 0 becomes `None`; levels 1/2/3 become `Some` with `has_basic=true`, `has_256=(level>=2)`, `has_16m=(level>=3)`. `on()` recomputes; `on_cached()` uses two independent process-static `OnceLock<Option<ColorLevel>>` slots, one per stream, caching **absence as well as presence**. A committed assertion changes `CLICOLOR` after caching and verifies that only `on()` changes. This API returns capability flags, not style spans. [Translation/cache](https://github.com/zkat/supports-color/blob/26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6/src/lib.rs#L69-L80), [cache implementation](https://github.com/zkat/supports-color/blob/26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6/src/lib.rs#L154-L183), [cache assertion](https://github.com/zkat/supports-color/blob/26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6/src/lib.rs#L244-L258).

#### `ansi-to-tui`: produced spans and persistent style

Represent the parser state as `(foreground, background, underline_color, add, remove)`. **Unset** means `None`; **Reset** means `Some(Color::Reset)`. `add` and `remove` are separate modifier masks, not merely the current visible modifiers. Codex enables Ratatui’s `underline-color` feature, so the underline-color field exists even though this parser implements no underline-color-setting SGR. [Codex feature](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/Cargo.toml#L409-L413), [feature forwarding](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui/Cargo.toml#L93-L102), [style fields](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/style.rs#L239-L270).

| Operation                      | Exact state change                                            |
| ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Start one `into_text()` call   | All colors unset; both masks empty                            |
| Full reset, SGR 0 or empty SGR | All three colors Reset; `add=∅`; `remove=ALL`                 |
| Add modifier `M`               | `add := add ∪ M`; `remove := remove − M`                      |
| Remove modifier `M`            | `add := add − M`; `remove := remove ∪ M`                      |
| Set foreground/background      | Replace only that color; preserve other colors and both masks |

`ALL` is precisely BOLD, DIM, ITALIC, UNDERLINED, SLOW_BLINK, RAPID_BLINK, REVERSED, HIDDEN, CROSSED_OUT. Reset therefore actively suppresses inherited modifiers; it is not equivalent to a fresh unstyled span. Patching selects each incoming non-unset color and combines masks by removing incoming removals from additions, then adding incoming additions, and symmetrically updating removals. [Initial state](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L89-L101), [modifier set](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/style.rs#L103-L114), [new/reset](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/style.rs#L298-L321), [mask operations](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/style.rs#L408-L434), [patch](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/style.rs#L471-L487).

The owned-text algorithm is:

1. Split a physical line **before parsing escapes**, stopping at either CR or LF; consume CRLF as one separator, otherwise consume one CR or LF.
2. Repeatedly consume at most one leading SGR/ignored escape, then take text up to the next ESC or line boundary. Decode UTF-8 and allocate an owned span containing that run with the accumulated style.
3. Update the state even when the run is empty, but emit only nonempty spans. Do not merge adjacent equal-style spans separated by escapes.
4. Carry the final style into the next physical line, including across empty lines. Only style survives: partial escape syntax does not.

Each `Line` and the enclosing `Text` retain default container style/alignment; accumulated style is stored on spans. [Parser loop](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L89-L145), [span creation](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L173-L198), [line construction](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/text/line.rs#L623-L629), [text construction](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/text/text.rs#L647-L653).

An empty input produces **one line with zero spans**. A terminal separator does not create an additional final line: `"A\n"` gives one line, `"A\n\n"` gives two, and `"\r\n"` gives one empty line. Bare CR starts another line; it does not overwrite prior text. Tabs remain literal `"\t"` inside dependency spans, with no expansion, tab-stop calculation, wrapping, width budget, or truncation. The separate Codex single-line wrapper substitutes exactly four U+0020 spaces per tab before parsing and starts a fresh parser call; it cannot inherit SGR from an earlier invocation. [Line/separator algorithm](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L89-L145), [literal-run predicate](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L173-L198), [Codex tab wrapper](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/ansi-escape/src/lib.rs#L6-L38).

#### Accepted SGR grammar and exact style effects

The only style introducer is the literal byte prefix `"\x1b["`; the terminator must be lowercase `"m"`. The parser accepts zero or more items followed by that terminator. In the grammar below, `ESC` means byte `0x1b`, and juxtaposition inserts no spaces:

```text
SGR      := ESC "[" ITEM* "m"
ITEM     := CODE [";"]                         when CODE is not 38 or 48
          | CODE [";"] COLOR [";"]             when CODE is 38 or 48
COLOR    := MODE ";" [";"] PAYLOAD
PAYLOAD  := BYTE ";" BYTE ";" BYTE             when MODE's value is 2
          | BYTE                              when MODE's value is 5
```

`CODE` and `BYTE` greedily parse unsigned decimal `u8`, range 0–255; leading zeroes are accepted, signs/whitespace are not, and overflow fails rather than clamps. `MODE` greedily parses signed decimal `i64`, accepting a leading `+`/`-`, but only values **2** and **5** succeed. Optional separators do not stop greedy number parsing. These details come from the locked `nom` functions, not a terminal standard. [SGR framing/items/colors](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L244-L257), [item/color grammar](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L280-L324), [signed/unsigned decimal parsing](https://github.com/rust-bakery/nom/blob/2cec1b3e4c9ccac62c902d60c00de6d1549ccbe1/src/character/complete.rs#L734-L867).

Consequences: `"\x1b[31;m"` is valid red; `"\x1b[;m"` and `"\x1b[31;;1m"` are invalid, not omitted-parameter resets. Both `"\x1b[38;2;;1;2;3m"` and the unusual `"\x1b[38+2;1;2;3m"` are accepted RGB `(1,2,3)`. Colon-form colors/underlines, spaces, out-of-range components, and incomplete RGB triplets do not parse as SGR. A failed sequence applies **none** of its partially parsed items; it enters the generic escape-discard path below. [Grammar and fallback](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L229-L324).

Items in a valid SGR apply left-to-right. All unmentioned state fields stay unchanged. [Style conversion](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L37-L87), [numeric dispatch](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/code.rs#L74-L138).

| SGR item                   | Effect                                                                                                                           |
| -------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 0; also no items (`ESC[m`) | Full reset described above                                                                                                       |
| 1 / 2 / 3 / 4              | Add BOLD / DIM / ITALIC / UNDERLINED respectively                                                                                |
| 5 / 6                      | Add SLOW_BLINK / RAPID_BLINK respectively; neither automatically removes the other                                               |
| 7 / 8 / 9                  | Add REVERSED / HIDDEN / CROSSED_OUT respectively; foreground/background are not swapped or erased in the span                    |
| 22                         | Remove both BOLD and DIM                                                                                                         |
| 23 / 24                    | Remove ITALIC / UNDERLINED respectively                                                                                          |
| 25                         | Remove both SLOW_BLINK and RAPID_BLINK                                                                                           |
| 27 / 28 / 29               | Remove REVERSED / HIDDEN / CROSSED_OUT respectively                                                                              |
| 39 / 49                    | Set foreground/background respectively to explicit Reset; preserve modifiers                                                     |
| 30–37 / 40–47              | Set foreground/background respectively to ordered named colors: Black, Red, Green, Yellow, Blue, Magenta, Cyan, Gray             |
| 90–97 / 100–107            | Set foreground/background respectively to DarkGray, LightRed, LightGreen, LightYellow, LightBlue, LightMagenta, LightCyan, White |
| 38 / 48 with mode 5        | Set foreground/background respectively to `Indexed(n)`, including indices 0–15; no named-color conversion                        |
| 38 / 48 with mode 2        | Set foreground/background respectively to `Rgb(r,g,b)`; no capability-dependent quantization                                     |
| Every other accepted code  | No effect, including 10–21, 26, 50–89, and 108–255                                                                               |

In particular, **21 does nothing**: although numeric dispatch names it `BoldOff`, conversion has no matching effect. It neither disables bold nor enables double underline. Codes 58/59 do not set/reset underline color. Subsequent items nevertheless retain their ordinary meanings: `"\x1b[58;5;1mX"` produces `"X"` with SLOW_BLINK+BOLD and all colors unset, rather than setting underline color 1. Unsupported codes are ignored individually only after the entire SGR passes the grammar. [Dispatch including 21](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/code.rs#L74-L138), [exhaustive implemented effects](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L37-L87), [unknown-code assertion](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/tests.rs#L153-L175).

#### OSC, other controls, and malformed escapes

When SGR parsing fails, the fallback consumes bytes according to this table, without changing style. Consumption is bounded by the physical line already extracted, **not** by the whole input. [Fallback implementation](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L229-L278).

| Prefix at current position                    | Bytes discarded                                                                                                                 |
| --------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `"\x1b["`                                     | Prefix, everything through the first ASCII letter `[A-Za-z]`, including that letter; if no letter occurs, the rest of this line |
| `"\x1b]"`                                     | Prefix, everything through the first BEL (`0x07`), including BEL; if none occurs, the rest of this line                         |
| Any other ESC, including final standalone ESC | **Only ESC**; the following byte is left for normal processing                                                                  |
| No ESC                                        | Literal text until ESC/CR/LF                                                                                                    |

The CSI fallback does **not** recognize the full standard final-byte range `0x40–0x7e`; its stopping predicate is specifically ASCII alphabetic. OSC has **BEL termination only**: it does not recognize ST (`"\x1b\\"`). Consequently, an ST-terminated OSC 8 hyperlink without a later BEL can swallow the link label and all remaining text on that line. No hyperlink target, OSC title, clipboard operation, or palette modification becomes metadata or style. [Fallback](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L259-L278), [exact alphabetic predicate](https://github.com/rust-bakery/nom/blob/2cec1b3e4c9ccac62c902d60c00de6d1549ccbe1/src/traits.rs#L691-L699).

DCS/APC/PM/SOS strings and two-character/charset escapes have no dedicated parser. For example `"\x1bPdata\x1b\\"` leaves literal `"Pdata"` and `"\\"` spans. Outside discarded escapes, NUL, BEL, TAB, backspace, vertical tab, form feed, DEL, and Unicode C1 controls are retained in span contents: there is no general control sanitization, bell execution, cursor motion, erase, or screen-buffer simulation. U+009B is not recognized as an alternate CSI introducer. These claims concern produced text, not what a later terminal writer might do with control characters. [Literal-run predicate](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L173-L198), [escape dispatch](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L259-L278), [explicit no-overwrite assertion](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/tests.rs#L102-L111).

Malformed escape strings do not normally reach Codex’s panic branches. In this locked implementation, `text()` returns `Ok`, while the line/span loops stop on parser errors rather than propagating them; `into_text()` also discards the returned remainder. Valid UTF-8 `&str` input, as supplied by Codex, is consumed using the tolerant fallback. Even the broader dependency byte API should not be treated as a strict validator: a UTF-8 error in a literal run stops that line’s span loop, silently dropping its remaining text, rather than returning the declared UTF-8 error. This follows from control flow, not from the crate’s error-type documentation. [Owned entry point](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/lib.rs#L122-L134), [outer/line loops](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L89-L145), [UTF-8 parsing](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L173-L198), [Codex error branches](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/121f91fd5d9dc66017866ce9bdc49f1e182721df/codex-rs/ansi-escape/src/lib.rs#L40-L57).

#### Worked dependency projections

The following show `into_text()` output **before** Codex adds prefixes, DIM, wrapping, or raw-text projection. Quoted strings use escape notation: `"\t"` is one tab; `" "` is one U+0020. `D` means the initial all-unset/empty-mask state; `R` means full reset, including explicit reset colors and `remove=ALL`. Unmentioned fields retain the stated starting state.

| Input                                     | Exact emitted line/span result                                                                                                                                                          |
| ----------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `"A\x1b[31mB\r\nC"`                       | Line 1: `"A"` D, `"B"` D with foreground Red. Line 2: `"C"` D with foreground Red.                                                                                                      |
| `"\x1b[1;31mA\x1b[22mB\x1b[39mC"`         | One line: `"A"` foreground Red, add BOLD; `"B"` foreground Red, add empty, remove BOLD+DIM; `"C"` foreground Reset, add empty, remove BOLD+DIM. All backgrounds/underline colors unset. |
| `"\x1b[0;31mR\x1b[1mB"`                   | `"R"`: R with foreground Red. `"B"`: foreground Red, background/underline Reset, add BOLD, remove ALL except BOLD.                                                                      |
| `"\x1b[1mA\x1b[21mB"`                     | Two separate spans `"A"`, `"B"`, each D with add BOLD.                                                                                                                                  |
| `"a\tb"`                                  | One span containing exactly `"a\tb"`, D; no generated spaces.                                                                                                                           |
| `"A\x1b[31;;1mB"`                         | Two spans `"A"`, `"B"`, both D; invalid SGR is discarded atomically.                                                                                                                    |
| `"A\x1b[1@BC"`                            | Two spans `"A"`, `"C"`, both D; fallback consumes through the letter `B`, not through `@`.                                                                                              |
| `"A\x1b]8;;url\x07B\x1b]8;;\x07C"`        | Three spans `"A"`, `"B"`, `"C"`, all D; no link metadata.                                                                                                                               |
| `"A\x1b]8;;url\x1b\\B\x1b]8;;\x1b\\C\nD"` | Line 1 contains only `"A"` D; line 2 contains `"D"` D. All of the first line after `A` is swallowed.                                                                                    |

The first row is a committed full-object assertion; reset, modifier removals, named/indexed/RGB colors, and empty-span suppression also have full-object assertions. The remaining edge cases are direct derivations of the inspected parser and mask operations, not executed Rust tests or terminal screenshots. [Cross-line assertion](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/tests.rs#L113-L122), [reset/empty-span/modifier assertions](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/tests.rs#L160-L307), [color assertions](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/tests.rs#L309-L497), [literal/escape derivation](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/parser.rs#L173-L324), [mask derivation](https://github.com/ratatui/ratatui/blob/e665c36cb14752a61cd777fbd06dbef8474f2add/ratatui-core/src/style.rs#L408-L434).

Neither dependency accepts pending/success/error/abort/replay state or a compact/transcript/raw mode. Given the same input and environment/cache state, they apply the same procedures; family-specific projections and lifecycle rules belong to their Codex callers. [Capability API](https://github.com/zkat/supports-color/blob/26ad8d206ad01ba7d6b4ad10d52f8f584aa68ec6/src/lib.rs#L154-L183), [ANSI API](https://github.com/ratatui/ansi-to-tui/blob/585c83c932c70cd0a9adec6270e1c5306a3929a3/src/lib.rs#L90-L134).

The following **Default syntax theme data** subsection separately specifies the decoded `two-face 0.5.1` bundle, including both default palettes and every ordered scope rule.

### Default syntax theme data

#### Pinned artifact and extraction
